The component receipts themselves are separate finite-volume producer statements; this proposition does not prove them from the charged-configuration hypotheses.

18.25.1Constructing the charged coefficient from its local pieces

We give the finite construction which precedes the proposition. This also fixes which nonlinear projections occur in its six terms. All pieces are on the same finite-volume representation and use its threshold \(\theta\); write \(H = W_1 (t)\) and put

\(\displaystyle c_0 = \theta^2 H^{- 2}, \qquad c_1 = \theta^2 H^{1 / 8}, \qquad \epsilon_0 = \theta^2 t^{- 1 / 100} .\)

Let \(\Pi_P f\) denote the weighted local coefficient of a regular observable \(f\) on a support \(P\) containing the section footprint. Its readout constant \(r \geqslant 1\) and residual constant \(a \geqslant 1\) are fixed at the stage. If the section bound is \(p \geqslant 0\), set \(K = (1 + p) r + a\).

First, the elementary local coefficient is an actual observable. Set

\(\displaystyle z = \Pi_P f, \qquad h = f - \text{present}_{\theta} (z), \qquad Q_f = \text{primary}_{\theta} (z) + \boldsymbol{1}_{\{P \text{healthy} \}} h.\)

Here the primary includes its own footprint mask. On a configuration healthy on \(P\), the two masks are one and \(Q_f = f\). The residual has zero local coefficient by construction. If the healthy derivative bound of \(f\) is \(u \geqslant 0\), coefficient recovery and residual subtraction give

\(\displaystyle |z|_{\text{coeff}} \leqslant ru, \qquad |h|_{\text{healthy}} \leqslant au.\)

Put \(M_P (u) = e^{\alpha \text{cov} (P)} u\), where \(\alpha\) is the support exponent. The coefficient with coordinate \(z\), one regular residual \(h\) on \(P\), and zero forced part has coefficient-plus-remainder bound at most

\(\displaystyle ru + M_P (au) \leqslant (r + a) M_P (u)\)

and pointwise exponential-entry bound at most \(((1 + p) r + a) u \leqslant KM_P (u)\). These inequalities use \(\text{cov} (P) \geqslant 0\) and the nonnegative support exponent \(\alpha\) (in the application \(\alpha \geqslant 4\)), hence \(M_P (u) \geqslant u\). The construction is measurable, gauge invariant and depends only on the links and healthy flags on \(P\): these facts hold for the local projection, its primary and the masked residual separately. Adding such coefficients adds both bounds and their exponential entries exactly. Thus this is a construction of one coefficient, not a choice of its coordinate alone.

For each direction \(b\), let \(f_b^0, f_b^1, f_b^2, f_b^3\) be respectively the local residual, the distinguished current term, the truncated loop term and the Taylor remainder of the selected local decomposition. Their healthy derivative bounds on the common patch \(P\) are respectively \(c_0, c_1, c_1, c_0\). Let \(g_{b, j}\), \(j \in I_b\), be its finite additional terms, supported on \(P_{b, j}\) in the same enclosing patch, with healthy bounds \(u_{b, j}\) and

\(\displaystyle \sum_{j \in I_b} M_{P_{b, j}} (u_{b, j}) \leqslant \epsilon_0 . \) (18.233)

Construct the elementary coefficient of \(f_b^0 + f_b^1 - f_b^2 + f_b^3\), with derivative bound \(2 c_0 + 2 c_1\), and add those of the \(g_{b, j}\). Denote the resulting coefficient by \(X_b\). The healthy observable and its coordinate are exactly

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rlll} Q_{X_b} & = f_b^0 + f_b^1 - f_b^2 + f_b^3 + \sum_j g_{b, j} & & \text{on the common healthy patch},\\ X_b .x & = \Pi_P f_b^0 + \Pi_P f_b^1 - \Pi_P f_b^2 + \Pi_P f_b^3 + \sum_j \Pi_{P_{b, j}} g_{b, j} . & & \end{array}\)

The second equality uses linearity of the local Taylor projection on regular functions on the same patch; it makes no assertion of global linearity for different nonlinear local charts. Both bounds are at most

\(\displaystyle K \{M_P (2 c_0 + 2 c_1) + \epsilon_0 \} .\)

The fixed patch cover bound gives \(M_P (1) \leqslant A\) with \(A = \exp (\alpha \hspace{0.17em} \text{TileNo})\). For large \(t\), \(\epsilon_0 \leqslant c_0 \leqslant c_1\) and therefore

\(\displaystyle K \{M_P (2 c_0 + 2 c_1) + \epsilon_0 \} \leqslant (4 A + 1) K \theta^2 H^{1 / 8} \leqslant \theta^2 H^{1 / 6} .\)

Indeed the last ratio is \((4 A + 1) KH^{- 1 / 24} \to 0\), while \(\epsilon_0 / c_0 = t^{- 1 / 100} H^2 \to 0\). The sum of added coordinates has size at most \((r + a) \epsilon_0 \leqslant K \epsilon_0 \leqslant c_0\). Also \(rc_0 \leqslant \theta^2 H^{- 1}\) eventually. Thus the local residual, Taylor remainder, and added-coordinate terms have the bounds asserted in (18.231)– (18.232). These are fixed-stage estimates. To use them along increasing stages, choose the threshold separately at each stage, simultaneously for the finitely many grades at most five and all its fixed readout constants. Lemma 18.11 gives the monotone delay envelope. The selected paid schedule meets these thresholds before the period, volume and finite truncations are chosen; this is not an assertion that unbounded stage constants are controlled by one fixed-stage tail. The coefficient has zero forced part. Its density is consequently the background density times its own exponential entry. The zero paid forced response has attainment cost zero, hence at most \(t\). This supplies the atomic certificate used for the finite-volume transfer in Subsection 18.11, with charge \(\theta^2 H^{1 / 6}\).

To obtain the six-term decomposition of the proposition, use the exact loop splitting

\(\displaystyle f_b^2 = k_b - d_b\)

on this same patch, where \(d_b\) has healthy derivative bound \(c_0\). Define

\(\displaystyle W_b = \Pi_P f_b^0, \quad B'_b = \Pi_P f_b^1, \quad K'_b = \Pi_P k_b, \quad D'_b = \Pi_P d_b, \quad E_b = \Pi_P f_b^3, \quad L_b = \sum_j \Pi_{P_{b, j}} g_{b, j} .\)

Local projection of the splitting gives \(X = W + B' - K' + D' + E + L\). The \(D'\) bound follows from \(rc_0 \leqslant \theta^2 H^{- 1}\) as well. The coefficient charge bounds \(|X_b .x|_{\text{coeff}}\) by \(\theta^2 H^{1 / 6}\); taking the maximum over directions proves the full array size estimate, rather than summing that estimate four times.

The two remaining flux statements have a different origin from these size bounds. The local radial reading identifies the normalized flux of \(\Pi_P f\) with one twelfth of the cubic current probe of \(f\). Thus the loop cancellation and distinguished-current comparison needed here are

\(\displaystyle \mathfrak{H} (k) = 0, \qquad |\mathfrak{H}(f^1) - m| \leqslant t^{- 30}, \qquad \ell (\theta^{- 2} \Pi_P f) =\mathfrak{H} (f) / 12. \) (18.234)

They give exactly the two identities in (18.230), with \(h =\mathfrak{H} (f^1)\). In particular the cancellation concerns \(k\), not \(f^2\): the small loop correction \(d\) must remain among the four errors. A bound on \(f^2\) alone would not prove the flux identity.

The following subsections construct the remaining local bounds and identities on these same fields: the corrected-loop cancellation and counterterm size, the actual nonlinear localization, the support-weighted tail, and the fifth-order residual and finite-difference remainder. They use the selected history's inherited action equation and local analytic probes. The exact finite tensor corrections are displayed below, so the trace and antisymmetric calibration identities are not additional assumptions. Once these constructions are combined, the coefficient and all its bounds above are produced by the displayed finite sum.

18.25.2Why the corrected loop has zero current bound

The cancellation in (18.234) can be checked before estimating any norms. A quadratic loop word has two plaquette legs, at offsets \(o_{j, 0}, o_{j, 1}\), and a color coefficient \(c_j\). All sums below can be taken separately for each pair of plaquette orientations and each pair of color indices. Put \(\delta_j = o_{j, 1} - o_{j, 0}\). The corrected finite loop family is chosen with

\(\displaystyle \sum_j c_j = 0, \qquad \sum_j c_j \delta_j = 0. \) (18.235)

These conditions have a concrete finite construction: subtract the local zeroth-moment word and the first-moment difference words from the original finite family, separately in each orientation/color component. A difference word with offsets \(0, e_a\), minus the word with offsets \(0, 0\), has zero zeroth moment and first moment \(e_a\). Taking its coefficient to be the \(a\)th component of the original first moment, and the local word's coefficient to be the original zeroth moment, cancels both moments exactly. The original loop equals the corrected family minus its added counterterms. The color coefficients of every added word are linear combinations of the original invariant color coefficients, so they remain invariant. These counterterms are the observable denoted \(d\) above; they cannot be discarded when estimating its size.

Let \(F\) be the translated and signed-averaged sum of the corrected words. At the identity configuration every curvature factor is zero, so \(F (0) = DF (0) = 0\). Write \(dA\) for the linear plaquette curl of a link variation \(A\). Differentiating the product of two curvature factors twice therefore gives, before signed averaging,

\(\displaystyle D^2 F (0) [A, B] = \sum_{j, z} c_j ((dA) (z + o_{j, 0}) (dB) (z + o_{j, 1}) + (dB) (z + o_{j, 0}) (dA) (z + o_{j, 1})) .\)

The displayed products retain their prescribed color contractions. Suppose \(A\) has bounded support and \(dB\) is affine on the support enlarged by the word lengths. Translate the first leg to \(z\). The inner sum of the first term is a multiple of

\(\displaystyle \sum_j c_j \{b_0 + L (z) + L (\delta_j)\} = (b_0 + L (z)) \sum_j c_j + L \left( \sum_j c_j \delta_j \right) = 0.\)

The other term has offsets \(- \delta_j\) and vanishes for the same reason. Nonwrapping of the enlarged patch justifies these ordinary integer translations; it avoids replacing an affine function by a discontinuous periodic representative. Signed averaging preserves the conclusion, since it preserves support and the property of having affine curl. Thus

\(\displaystyle D^2 F (0) [A, B] = 0 \quad \text{if $A$ is supported in the patch and $dB$ is affine there.} \) (18.236)

Now let \(Y_b\) be the local gauge-covariant vector field of the chosen current, vanishing at the identity, and let \(k_b = DF \hspace{0.17em} Y_b\). The support of \(DY_b (0) A\) is contained in the same patch. Twice applying the product rule, using \(DF (0) = 0\) and \(Y_b (0) = 0\), gives

\(\displaystyle D^2 k_b (0) [A, B] = D^2 F (0) [A, DY_b (0) B] + D^2 F (0) [B, DY_b (0) A] .\)

For the polynomial link probes of degrees zero, one and two entering the local cubic current test, their curls are affine. Each term consequently vanishes by (18.236) and symmetry of the Hessian. The constant-direction terms require a separate argument. The function \(k_b = DF \hspace{0.17em} Y_b\) is gauge invariant, since \(F\) is gauge invariant and \(Y_b\) is gauge covariant. Moreover \(k_b (0) = Dk_b (0) = 0\). Differentiating gauge invariance once in a gauge parameter and once in an arbitrary variation therefore says that \(D^2 k_b (0)\) annihilates every infinitesimal gauge direction. On the nonwrapping patch a constant link variation is such a direction: use the gauge parameter linear in the lattice coordinate and extend it outside the patch. This kills the \((0, 3)\) and \((3, 0)\) terms without claiming that a cubic probe has affine curl. The radial current reading is a finite linear combination of these Hessian probes: its cubic spatial coefficient is the polarization of the pairs of spatial degrees \((0, 3), (1, 2), (2, 1), (3, 0)\), with the constant-curl terms removed by the gauge identity. The middle two pairs are exactly the quadratic link probes just treated. Hence every term of its current reading is zero and \(\mathfrak{H} (k) = 0\). This proof uses moment cancellation of the corrected loop, rather than smallness or a limiting Ward identity.

18.25.3The exact reference-profile bound

The stress probe and its divergence are read in grades four and five, respectively. Grade four is particularly useful here: its nonconstant invariant labels are quadratic in curvature, so the local reading is determined by its Hessian on constant-curvature radial jets. A translated finite difference has zero such reading. Indeed translation leaves these Hessian tests unchanged, and the difference of the two equal readings is zero. This statement concerns the local grade-four reading, not the observable itself.

To make the same-object identities explicit, let \(\tilde{J}\) be the componentwise localized tensor of the selected field, and let \(Q_i\) and \(A_{abi}\) be its localized trace and antisymmetric auxiliary currents. Write \(\Delta_i\) for the unit translated difference. The corrected local tensor \(M\) keeps \(\tilde{J}_{ab}\) for \(a < b\) and \(\tilde{J}_{aa}\) for \(a \ne 0\), and is defined on the remaining entries by

\(\displaystyle M_{ba} = M_{ab} - \sum_i \Delta_i A_{abi} \quad (a < b), \qquad M_{00} = \sum_i \Delta_i Q_i - \sum_{i \ne 0} M_{ii} .\)

Consequently, before any projection or averaging,

\(\displaystyle \sum_i M_{ii} = \sum_i \Delta_i Q_i, \qquad M_{ab} - M_{ba} = \sum_i \Delta_i A_{abi} \quad (a < b) .\)

All these auxiliary fields and their translated differences use the same finite nonwrapping support and the same healthy regular construction. Thus linearity of the weighted local projection applies to these exact identities. Applying the preceding zero-reading observation proves that its grade-four reading is symmetric and trace free. Signed averaging preserves both statements: each diagonal receives the square of the sign, and permutations only reorder the trace sum. The grade-four classification in Lemma 16.2 therefore writes the averaged reading uniquely as the three-parameter nonconstant stress array \(\textsf{S} (a)\), \(a \in \mathbb{R}^3\). Reading the local divergence commutes with taking the grade-five derivative: this follows by differentiating the finite radial Taylor polynomial and comparing each jet coefficient. Consequently its vector reading is the same \(Va\) used in (18.218); it is not independently calibrated.

The trace and vector bound in these coordinates are

\(\displaystyle \sum_b \textsf{S} (a)_{bb} = 4 (a_0 + 4 a_1) \mathcal{O}, \qquad \rho (Va) = - 4 (a_0 + 4 a_1) .\)

Since \(\mathcal{O} \ne 0\) for a nontrivial simple gauge group, the zero trace gives \(a_0 + 4 a_1 = 0\), hence \(\rho (Va) = 0\) exactly at finite volume. For the two elementary correction tensors the local reading has the fixed triples \(a^0, a^1\), so their bounds equal \(\rho (Va^P)\) exactly. This proves the exact bound part of (18.218) without a quantitative limit for the stress coefficients.

For the qualitative stress calibration, let \(J_{bc}\) be the unmodified stress probe and \(\tilde{J}_{bc}\) its local version. On each fixed constant-curvature test pair the selected local estimates give

\(\displaystyle | D^2 J_{bc} (0) [u, v] - D^2 (- \textsf{S}_{\text{Maxwell}})_{bc} (0) [u, v] | \leqslant t^{- 100}, \qquad | D^2 (J_{bc} - \tilde{J}_{bc}) (0) [u, v] | \leqslant 16 t^{- 30} .\)

The factor \(16\) is the product of the two factor-four link bounds for a constant-curvature radial test. There are finitely many tests determining the grade-four frame: evaluation on all radial jets is injective, and in finite dimension a finite subset already has zero common kernel. Their inverse on its image has finite operator norm. The triangle inequality therefore gives convergence of the entire local coefficient to the negative Maxwell coefficient. Applying the continuous inverse of \(a \mapsto \textsf{S} (a)\) gives \(a \to (- 1, 1 / 4, - 1)\), uniformly on any sets on which these two test bounds hold. This is precisely the qualitative reference limit used in the weighted estimate; its weighted bound uses the exact zero above and does not multiply this qualitative error by \(A_{1 / 5}\).

18.25.4The localization error in the current comparison

The current comparison uses a shrinking analytic width, so its loss cannot be treated as a fixed constant. At scale \(t\), the width is \(a = (\log t)^{- 1}\) and the localization radius is \(R = 1000 \lceil (\log t)^2 \rceil\). Here is the nonlinear localization estimate for the actual localizer. Write \(F\) for the scalar reading of a gauge-invariant local field, \(J_4 F\) for its Taylor polynomial of degree four, \(S_R\) for restriction of link coordinates to distance at most \(R\), and \(W\) for the degree-four local gauge-coordinate map. These readings are analytic at the zero field and satisfy \(F = F \circ \text{exactP}_o\) as germs there: this is the local gauge reconstruction identity of the constructed invariant field. The Taylor polynomial of that reconstruction is \(W\). Thus the defining coordinate identity gives equality of four-jets

\(\displaystyle F - (J_4 F \circ S_R) \circ W \equiv_4 (J_4 F - J_4 F \circ S_R) \circ W.\)

This is an identity of jets, not an assertion of equality of the full nonlinear fields. In a coordinate derivative indexed by links \((e_1, \ldots, e_j)\), \(j \leqslant 4\), the jet in parentheses is zero if \(\max_i d (o, e_i) \leqslant R\), and otherwise is the original derivative of \(F\). Thus every retained term has at least one input outside the radius.

For completeness, the local coordinate map has derivative row sums, through order four and on a radius-\(g\) set, bounded by \(B (1 + g)^L \exp (4 (a / 100) (g + 2))\), with \(B, L\) depending only on the gauge group and derivative order. To see the volume independence of these bounds, a coordinate at distance \(g\) depends only on coordinates within radius \(4 (g + 2)\). There are at most \(2 (2 \cdot 4 (g + 2) + 1)^4 \dim (\text{Loc})\) such coordinates. Its derivatives through order four have a polynomial bound in \(g\) by the finite product rule for the local gauge-coordinate map. Multiplying that bound by the fourth power of this coordinate count and by the polynomial spatial weight gives \(B (1 + g)^L\); the exponential weight contributes the displayed exponential. Thus the count concerns only this finite neighborhood. The chain rule is a sum over the ordered partitions of \(j \leqslant 4\). For each partition, there are at most four such row factors. Using \(g > R\), their product, together with the output weight of width \(a / 100\), is bounded by the original input weight of width \(a\), times

\(\displaystyle B^4 e^{100} \mathfrak{M} (4 L, a / 4) e^{- aR / 4} .\)

Here \(\mathfrak{M} (s, \eta) = e^{\eta} s! / \eta^s\) bounds \((1 + g)^s e^{- \eta g}\) for \(g \geqslant 0\) and \(\eta > 0\), by the exponential series. In particular \((1 + g)^{4 L} \leqslant \mathfrak{M} (4 L, a / 4) e^{ag / 4}\), and the remaining exponent satisfies \((a / 100) g + 16 (a / 100) (g + 2) + ag / 4 \leqslant ag + 100 - aR / 4\) for \(0 < a \leqslant 1\). In the following display \(\| \cdot \|_{a, \leqslant 4}\) denotes the maximum of the exponentially weighted coordinate-derivative sums at zero, through order four, with the degree-four polynomial spatial weight. It is not a supremum of derivatives over the healthy domain. Summing over the finitely many ordered partitions gives a constant \(D\) and an integer \(P\), independent of the volume, such that

\(\displaystyle \|F - F^R \|_{a / 100, \hspace{0.17em} \leqslant 4} \leqslant D\mathfrak{M} (P, a / 4) e^{- aR / 4} \|F\|_{a, \hspace{0.17em} \leqslant 4} . \) (18.237)

The scalar readings of the actual vector and tensor localizers are exactly \((J_4 F \circ S_R) \circ W\): this follows by substituting their linkwise polynomial definition and contracting the link index. Their zero and first derivatives vanish; thus the weighted estimate for orders two through four supplies all the input derivatives in the calculation. This proves the estimate for those localizers, without promoting a scalar convolution estimate to a nonlinear one.

The selected input bound is at most \(CH^q\) for a fixed \(q\). The moment bound above is a fixed polynomial in \(a^{- 1}\). Since \(aR / 4 \geqslant 250 \log t\), the right side of (18.237) is \(t^{- 250 + o (1)}\), and is eventually at most \(t^{- 98}\). Only orders at most four are used here; they suffice for the second-derivative current test and its one divergence derivative.

To make the current test estimate explicit, write \(Y_b\) for the original local vector field and \(Y_b^R\) for its localized field. The divergence operator defining the current has one more derivative and a finite weighted link sum. Applying the product rule and the preceding tail bound, with the analytic width decreased to \(a / 100\), gives an error at most \(t^{- 70}\) in the weighted derivative norm needed for this operator. The spare exponent absorbs all fixed derivative orders and polynomial support factors. The current probe is a fixed finite sum of spatial moments of its second derivatives. If \(c = a / (100 \cdot 5 \cdot 4 \cdot 5)\), the sums of the moment weights of degrees two and three are bounded by \(C (c^{- 1} + 1)^m\) for a fixed \(m\): sum the four-dimensional geometric series and differentiate it at most three times. Consequently

\(\displaystyle |\mathfrak{H}(\text{div} \hspace{0.17em} Y) -\mathfrak{H}(\text{div} \hspace{0.17em} Y^R) | \leqslant C (1 + \log t)^m t^{- 70} \leqslant t^{- 30}\)

for all sufficiently large \(t\). Subtracting the constant value of \(\text{div} \hspace{0.17em} Y^R\) does not change this test, which uses its second derivatives. This proves the distinguished-current error in (18.234) for the localized field. The same argument with the two constant-curvature directions gives the \(16 t^{- 30}\) local stress-probe error used above. The other stress-probe error, \(t^{- 100}\), is the chosen accuracy of the original finite-volume probe; the two bounds concern different comparisons.

18.25.5Paying the full reference observable

The reference profiles require their full observable, including its value on unhealthy configurations. They therefore use the following guarded construction rather than the zero-forced coefficient above. Let \(f\) be one of the reference tensor or divergence probes on \(P\), with global bound \(b = H^{1 / 50}\) and healthy derivative bound \(c = \theta^2 H^{1 / 8}\). These are the local regularity bounds for this profile. Set \(z = \Pi_P f\) and let \(h = f - \text{present}_{\theta} \hspace{0.17em} z\) be its zero-reading residual. The primary's footprint mask differs from the mask on \(P\); keep this difference by defining

\(\displaystyle q = f - \text{primary}_{\theta} \hspace{0.17em} z - \boldsymbol{1}_{\{P \text{healthy} \}} h.\)

Then the equality \(f = \text{primary}_{\theta} \hspace{0.17em} z + \boldsymbol{1}_{\{P \text{healthy} \}} h + q\) holds on every configuration. Here \(a = 1 + (1 + \text{readable}) r\) is the canonical atomic bound, so the local residual construction bounds \(h\) by \(ac\) on the healthy set and bounds the footprint-mask discrepancy by \(ac\) globally. Hence \(q\) is zero on the healthy set and \(|q| \leqslant b + ac\) everywhere. It is measurable, invariant, supported on \(P\) and depends only on its links and flags, so \(q\) is an admissible supported forced response. Its attainment bound is

\(\displaystyle e^{(\alpha + 2) \text{cov} (P)} (b + ac) .\)

The retained forced-activity expansion installs this supported response in the same background density. To recall its quantitative content, mark one copy of \(q\) on \(P\), expand the background activities and collect each nonempty subset meeting \(P\) into a supported forced slot, grouping by its union with \(P\). Untouched activities remain outside this slot. There is no empty-slot contribution: \(q\) vanishes when \(P\) is healthy. If the rooted forced budget is \(v\), their total absolute bound is at most

\(\displaystyle M_P (b + ac) (e^{|P|v} - 1) .\)

Indeed for \(j\) selected factors the rooted product sum is at most \((|P|v)^j / j\)!; sum over \(j \geqslant 1\). This is the same supported forcing expansion used by the atomic input of Subsection 18.11; all terms retain the same marked origin and its flags. Its exact product expansion makes the resulting coefficient density equal to \(\text{eval} \hspace{0.17em} f\), not just equal on the healthy set. For \(|P| v \leqslant 1\), \(e^{|P| v} - 1 \leqslant 2 |P| v\). In the remainder norm with tolerance \(\sqrt{v}\) the resulting bound is consequently at most \(2 |P| \sqrt{v} \hspace{0.17em} M_P (b + ac)\). Together with the ordinary residual and the coefficient this gives

\(\displaystyle |z|_{\text{coeff}} + \text{rem} \leqslant rc + M_P (ac) + 2 |P| \sqrt{v} \hspace{0.17em} M_P (b + ac) . \) (18.238)

This explains both the reserved support exponent \(\alpha + 2\) in the attainment bound and the extra masked-remainder term; dropping either would not give the reference atomic certificate.

Here the actual forced budget is \(v = \exp \{- \exp [(\log \log (t^{1 / 5}))^2]\}\), \(|P| = O ((\log t)^8)\) and \(\text{cov} (P) \leqslant \text{TileNo}\). The last term of (18.238) is smaller than every negative power of \(t\): take logarithms and use \(\exp [(\log \log (t^{1 / 5}))^2] / \log t \to \infty\). It is eventually at most \(\theta^2\). The other terms are at most \((r + Aa) \theta^2 H^{1 / 8}\), so the whole bound is at most \(\theta^2 H^{1 / 6}\) after the stage delay has been paid. Similarly

\(\displaystyle e^{(\alpha + 2) \text{TileNo}} (H^{1 / 50} + a \theta^2 H^{1 / 8}) \leqslant t\)

eventually. Thus each reference profile has the atomic charge and attainment bound used in (18.217), with its original full observable and the same weighted local coefficient. Taking the maximum of the finitely many thresholds covers all three profiles and their tensor and divergence entries.

18.25.6The weighted nonempty-support tail

The added supports in (18.233) are the nonempty subsets of the activity increments in the finite shifted product. Here is the estimate which keeps their support weights intact. Fix a marked link, let \(Q\) be the core support, and let \(X_i\) be the supports of its activity increments \(f_i\), all containing the marked site. Write \(p_i\) for their derivative bounds and \(A\) for the bound of the unshifted local factor. If the derivative order is \(k\), set \(c_k = 2^k \text{budC} (k)\). The product rule and the derivative series for \(e^{f_i} - 1\) give the bound

\(\displaystyle A \prod_{i \in I} c_k p_i\)

for the term with nonempty index set \(I\). The \(2^k\) factor pays the product rule; \(\text{budC} (k) p_i\) pays the exponential increment when \(p_i \leqslant 1\). Its support is \(Q \cup \bigcup_{i \in I} X_i\). Since these supports share their marked site, their covering numbers satisfy

\(\displaystyle \text{cov} (Q \cup \bigcup_{i \in I} X_i) \leqslant \text{cov} (Q) + \sum_{i \in I} \text{cov} (X_i) .\)

If \(\sum_i M_{X_i} (p_i) \leqslant \delta\) and \(c_k \delta \leqslant 1\), positivity and the exact finite-product expansion therefore give

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rl} \sum_{\varnothing \ne I} M_{Q \cup \cup_{i \in I} X_i} (A \prod_{i \in I} c_k p_i) & \leqslant M_Q (A) \left[ \prod_i (1 + c_k M_{X_i} (p_i)) - 1 \right]\\ & \leqslant M_Q (A) (e^{c_k \delta} - 1) \leqslant 2 c_k \delta M_Q (A) . \end{array}\)

This also proves regularity term by term: the increments and the local factor are regular, and the finite product is regular on the displayed union. No unweighted cardinality estimate is substituted for this sum.

For the actual shifted activity family, the individual bounds are the sum of the exterior-path increment bound and the local regular-activity increment bound. Summing them gives

\(\displaystyle \delta \leqslant \text{beamAmt} + \text{vat} \hspace{0.17em} (2 r + 1)^4 \text{hue} (t) \leqslant t^{- 109 / 100} .\)

Here \(r\) is the retained truncation radius. The exterior contribution contains \(e^{- \sigma R / 4}\), with fixed \(\sigma > 0\) and \(R = 1000 \lceil \log^2 t \rceil\), times fixed powers of \(t\) and logarithms, so is smaller than every negative power of \(t\). The regular contribution is bounded by \(t^{- 11 / 10 + o (1)}\): the radius factors are subpolynomial under the retained tower bound, and its activity receipt obeys \(\text{hue} (t) \leqslant t^{- 11 / 10 + o (1)}\). Thus both terms are bounded together by \(t^{- 109 / 100}\) after increasing the stage threshold. The local shifted factor, after the fixed derivative composition, has bound \(A \leqslant t^{- 1 / 2 + o (1)}\). Its support has at most \(\text{TileNo}\) covering blocks. These facts give the per-link, per-difference-node nonempty bound

\(\displaystyle S (t) \leqslant 2 c_k t^{- 109 / 100} e^{\alpha \text{TileNo}} t^{- 1 / 2 + o (1)} .\)

There are \(t^{o (1)}\) marked links, the fixed finite-difference weights have total absolute value \(\sum_{j < m} |w_j |\), and the inverse difference step is bounded by \(t^{1 / 2 + 1 / 24 + o (1)}\). Consequently the actual support sum, including the difference weights and normalization, is bounded by

\(\displaystyle t^{- 109 / 100 - 1 / 2 + 1 / 2 + 1 / 24 + o (1)} = t^{- 629 / 600 + o (1)} \leqslant t^{- 101 / 100} \leqslant \theta^2 t^{- 1 / 100} .\)

The last inequality is the retained threshold bound \(t^{- 1} \leqslant \theta^2\). The strict gap between \(629 / 600\) and \(101 / 100\) absorbs the fixed stage constants. This proves (18.233) for the finite activity family, uniformly in its finite truncations, with no additional factor \(H^2\).

18.25.7From the constructed probes to healthy derivative bounds

The local stress, its divergence, and the elementary correction probes are the entries and first spatial differences of the same localized polynomial field: the localized stress (Meld), the two fixed quadratic probes (Plint), the trace current (pluck) and the antisymmetric current (pluckD). This includes their tensor, single-derivative and divergence entries through grade five. Their local analytic construction gives derivative bounds \(A \leqslant H^{1 / 50}\) on translated link-coordinate balls of the fixed collar radius \(B \geqslant 1\). Each probe is gauge invariant, local, and has zero constant and linear Taylor coefficients at the flat field. The last property must be retained in passing to a healthy chart: an arbitrary bounded local observable would not have the factor \(\theta^2\).

The healthy axial chart has size \(s = \theta (1 + r)^p\), with \(r\) the support radius and \(p\) the fixed stage power. On this chart use the weighted derivative norm in which a \(j\)th derivative is multiplied by \(s^j\). Taylor's formula twice gives \(|f (su) | \leqslant C_B As^2\), and once gives \(s \|Df (su)\| \leqslant C_B As^2\). For \(j \geqslant 2\), the original bound gives \(s^j \|D^j f (su)\| \leqslant As^2\) since \(s \leqslant 1\). Thus the entire weighted norm through the retained order is at most \(C_B As^2\). Pullback through the healthy collar chart, by the finite chain rule, costs at most \(k! (1 + B)^k\). It follows that the healthy derivative bound is

\(\displaystyle k! (1 + B)^k C_B A \theta^2 (1 + r)^{2 p} .\)

For \(r = O (\log^2 t)\) the last polynomial is \(H^{o (1)}\). Since \(1 / 50 < 1 / 8\), the displayed expression is at most \(\theta^2 H^{1 / 8}\). At the origin of each translated coordinate chart, the original zeroth-order bound also gives the global estimate \(|f (U) | \leqslant A \leqslant H^{1 / 50}\). This proves the two distinct bounds used in Subsubsection 18.25.5 from the local probe bounds, on the original full observable.

For the distinguished divergence subtract its value at the flat field. Its first jet vanishes by global conjugation invariance and simplicity of the gauge Lie algebra: restricting that derivative to any one link gives an adjoint-invariant linear functional, which vanishes on all commutators, hence on the entire simple Lie algebra. The link-coordinate derivatives therefore all vanish. Subtracting the constant supplies the zero value, and does not alter this first-derivative argument. Its derivative bound before the subtraction is \(H^{1 / 200}\), and the subtracted constant has absolute value at most this same number. Hence \(A \leqslant 2 H^{1 / 200}\) suffices, and the same argument again gives the healthy bound \(\theta^2 H^{1 / 8}\). For the small loop counterterm the corresponding analytic bound is \(\text{countertermBound} (t)\); its decay is faster than the fixed \(H^{- 2}\) bound after the same polynomial chart factors. The healthy counterterm bound is consequently \(\theta^2 H^{- 2}\). Finally, if a residual or Taylor error has already been bounded in the axial chart by \(\theta^2 t^{- 1 / 100}\), the finite pullback factor gives

\(\displaystyle k! (1 + B)^k \theta^2 t^{- 1 / 100} \leqslant \theta^2 H^{- 2} .\)

All constants here belong to the selected finite stage and are paid by the same prior scale threshold as the atomic construction.

18.25.8Size of the moment counterterms

The zero and first moments used to correct the finite loop family are small for a reason independent of their exact cancellation. The infinite loop family of the selected retained path has exactly zero zeroth and first moments, by its path law. Restricting that same family to the radius \(R\) loses at most

\(\displaystyle e^{- \sigma R / 4} \mathfrak{M} (1, \sigma / 2) \text{pathBound} (t)\)

in the combined zeroth- and first-moment norm: insert the factor distance into the absolutely summable loop coefficients and spend half of the exponential decay to pay it. For each fixed \(t\), finite truncation of that summable family can be chosen so that its additional moment error is at most \(t^{- 100}\). The combined moment error is therefore bounded by \(t^{- 98}\) for all sufficiently large \(t\), uniformly after this finite truncation threshold.

The explicit correction in Subsubsection 18.25.2 depends linearly on these finitely many moments. Its words have length at most ten and fixed offsets \(0, e_a\). Differentiating any such word a fixed number of times costs a fixed constant; translating it over the support costs at most \(C (1 + R_{\text{supp}} (t))^4\), where \(R_{\text{supp}} (t) = 4 (R + 2)\) is the support radius for the translated corrections. Hence the analytic derivative bound is at most \(t^{- 95}\), after enlarging the stage threshold. Applying the localized vector field in the directional derivative adds only the fixed product-rule constant and its subpolynomial derivative bound. The resulting counterterm \(d_b\) has analytic bound at most \(t^{- 94}\), and in particular at most \(\text{countertermBound} (t) = t^{- 30}\). Subsubsection 18.25.7 now gives its healthy bound \(\theta^2 H^{- 2}\). This establishes smallness of the same finite counterterms which enforce the exact moments; no second family is chosen for the size bound.

18.25.9The fifth-order residual and finite-difference remainder

Let \(s = \theta (1 + r)^p\) be the same axial-chart scale. The action residual is the actual difference

\(\displaystyle K_b = \text{div} \hspace{0.17em} M_b - DF_{\text{main}} [Y_b^R], \qquad f_b^0 = \beta K_b .\)

Here the small four-jet input follows from the inherited action identity, not from locality alone. Let \(J, Y, F\) be the original stress, current and full main interaction, and let \(F_L\) be the same main interaction restricted to the retained finite family and radius. In this paragraph \(\text{div} \hspace{0.17em} M\) is the spatial finite-difference divergence of the stress, as opposed to the field-space divergence of \(Y\) in the current-bound calculation. Then exactly

\(\displaystyle \text{div} \hspace{0.17em} M - DF_L [Y^R] = (\text{div} \hspace{0.17em} J - DF [Y]) + \text{div} (M - J) + DF [Y - Y^R] + D (F - F_L) [Y^R] .\)

The first summand has weighted four-jet bound at most \(t^{- 100}\) by the inherited action equation of the selected field. The second has bound at most \(t^{- 70}\) times a fixed polynomial in \(a^{- 1}\) by the localization estimate. For the third, the weighted product rule pairs the localized-current tail \(t^{- 70}\) with the full action's link derivative bound through four further field derivatives (thus through five derivatives of the scalar action), bounded by a fixed power of \(H\) and \(a^{- 1}\). The retained action family has exactly this bound; its four-derivative bound for the scalar action alone would not suffice. Finally absolute summability of the action family permits its finite truncation error to be at most \(t^{- 100}\), and the radius restriction costs at most \(C\mathfrak{M} (P, \sigma / 2) e^{- \sigma R / 4}\) times the same fixed support bound. Multiplying this by the original-current bound plus its \(t^{- 70}\) tail bounds the last summand. All spatial contractions here are exponentially weighted link sums; their moment losses are polynomials in \(a^{- 1}\), independent of total volume. The sum is \(t^{- 70 + o (1)} + t^{- 100 + o (1)}\), hence at most \(t^{- 30}\) eventually. This proves the claimed origin four-jet bound for the exact \(K_b\) above. Meanwhile the local analytic construction bounds all derivatives through the required fixed order by \(CH^{q_0}\) for fixed \(C, q_0\) on the radius-\(Bs\) ball. Both statements concern the same finite main interaction: its finite truncation is chosen after the local four-jet comparison. For \(0 \leqslant j \leqslant 4\) and sufficiently large \(t\),

\(\displaystyle t^{- 30} \leqslant CH^{q_0} s^{5 - j}, \qquad t^{- 1} \leqslant s \leqslant 1.\)

Taylor's formula for \(D^j K_b (su)\) through degree \(4 - j\), with the fifth and higher derivatives bounded on the line segment, therefore bounds each scaled derivative by \(C_B CH^{q_0} s^5\). For \(j \geqslant 5\), the same bound follows directly from \(s^j \leqslant s^5\). The finite Taylor constants are the fixed fifth-order interpolation constant \(C_B\). Multiplying by \(| \beta | \leqslant 4 t\) gives

\(\displaystyle \| \beta K_b (s \hspace{0.17em} \cdot)\|_{C^k (B)} \leqslant 4 t \hspace{0.17em} C_B CH^{q_0} s^5 \leqslant \theta^2 t^{- 1 / 100} .\)

Indeed, after division by \(\theta^2\), the left side is \(t^{- 1 / 2}\) times a fixed power of \(H\) and \(1 + r\), hence is \(t^{- 1 / 2 + o (1)}\). This is the actual fifth-order residual estimate; the quadratic healthy-bound argument alone would not prove it.

For the finite-difference remainder let \(h = \theta t^{- 1 / 24}\) be the chosen step and \(m \geqslant 48\) its order. At each marked link and color, write the shifted integrand as the product of the local current factor and the exponential action increment. The latter increment is zero at zero shift. The derivative bound for that increment is \(Z (t) = 2 \hspace{0.17em} \text{shear} (t) C_B s^2 \leqslant H^c\). The segment has length at most \(\kappa_m h / s\), where \(\kappa_m\) depends only on the fixed finite-difference stencil. Thus \(Z (t) \kappa_m h / s \leqslant H^c \kappa_m t^{- 1 / 24} \leqslant 1\) eventually. Integrating its first derivative along this segment bounds the increment itself by one. The scalar exponential has bounded outer derivatives on \([- 1, 1]\); after composition its derivatives through order \(k + m + 1\) are bounded by fixed powers of \(H\), not by an absolute constant. The chain rule and the product rule therefore bound the full integrand by

\(\displaystyle C (t) s, \qquad C (t) \leqslant H^c\)

for some fixed \(c\), after including the local-current bound and the finite coordinate-chart factors. For clarity, the action-dependent composition factor contains \(\text{shear} (t) s^2\); since \(\text{shear} (t) \leqslant 5 tH^{1 / 50}\) and \(s^2 = t^{- 1} H^2 (1 + r)^{2 p}\), this factor is bounded by a fixed power of \(H\). All further powers are fixed by \(k + m + 1\).

The finite-difference weights cancel the Taylor coefficients through order \(m\). Taylor's integral remainder, differentiated up to order \(k\), then bounds the error at each marked link and color by \(C (t) s (h / s)^{m + 1}\). Summing over the actual finite support multiplies \(C (t)\) by at most \(O ((1 + r)^4) \dim \mathfrak{g}\), still a fixed power of \(H\). Since \(h / s \leqslant t^{- 1 / 24}\),

\(\displaystyle C (t) s (h / s)^{m + 1} = C (t) h (h / s)^m \leqslant t^{- 2 + o (1)} h \leqslant \theta^2 t^{- 1 / 100} h.\)

The observable \(f_b^3\) divides this Taylor error by \(h\). That division cancels the displayed factor \(h\) exactly, leaving the same axial bound \(\theta^2 t^{- 1 / 100}\) as for \(f_b^0\). The finite collar pullback established above therefore gives the healthy bound \(\theta^2 H^{- 2}\) for both errors. The finite-difference integrand estimate also proves the shifted local factor bound \(t^{- 1 / 2 + o (1)}\) used in Subsubsection 18.25.6: it is \(s\) times fixed powers of \(H\), before taking the Taylor remainder or dividing by \(h\).
